But for months now it’s been the same pattern—appointments moved, bills showing up wrong, messages supposedly not received or forgotten—and somehow all of it has the same effect: I’m kept from having the information I need, and I’m pushed out of decisions I should be involved in.\n\nSo yes, I think I’m being targeted in that sense. Maybe they wouldn’t use that word, but that’s what it feels like to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs you have about being deceived and targeted are based on facts that other people would agree with if they saw the same evidence?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think it’s based on facts. That’s what frustrates me, because people act like I’m just “feeling” something or being suspicious for no reason, but I’m talking about actual things that happened. My sister’s appointments have been moved, and I haven’t been told properly. Bills have come in with wrong amounts or details that don’t line up. People have said they would call me back and then they don’t, or they say they never got a message. Those are facts.\n\nNow, would other people agree with me about what it all means? That’s where everyone keeps backing away. They’ll say, “Oh, that could just be a mistake,” or “Offices get busy,” or “Maybe there was a misunderstanding.” But if they saw the whole pattern, not just one isolated incident, I think they would have to admit something is going on. Because it’s not one thing. It’s been months of the same kind of thing happening over and over, and the result is always the same: I’m kept out of the loop, I don’t get the information I need, and I’m pushed away from decisions I should be part of.\n\nSo yes, I believe if someone really looked at all of it together, honestly, they’d see what I’m seeing. They might not want to say it out loud, but they’d see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs of yours could be wrong, or are you absolutely certain they are true?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say absolutely certain in the way people mean, like I can prove every single person’s motive on paper. I know I can’t climb inside someone’s head. But could I be wrong about the overall pattern? Honestly… I don’t think so.\n\nThat’s the part that’s hard to explain without sounding like I’m being stubborn. I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the same things happen again and again—my sister’s appointments get moved, bills show up wrong, people say they’ll call me back and then they “forget” or suddenly never got the message. And every time I raise it, I’m told it’s just mistakes, or confusion, or I’m overreacting. But it always ends up with me not having the information I need, and me being pushed out of decisions I should be part of.\n\nSo, no, I’m not saying I have some signed document proving it. But I do know what I’m seeing. Maybe I could be wrong about one bill, or one phone call, or one appointment change. But all of it together? Over and over? I really don’t think I’m wrong about that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any situation or evidence that could change your mind about people trying to deceive or target you?\n\nPatient: I suppose if everything suddenly became completely transparent—if every appointment change was documented clearly, if the bills made sense, if people actually returned calls when they said they would, and if I was included in the decisions instead of finding things out afterward—then maybe I’d have to reconsider it.\n\nBut that’s not what has been happening. For months now it’s been the same pattern. My sister’s appointments get moved and I’m not told properly, bills come in wrong, people “forget” to call me back or say they never got the message, and then when I question it, I’m treated like I’m making something out of nothing. And everyone keeps saying, “Oh, it’s just mistakes,” but the mistakes always seem to work in the same direction. They keep information from me and push me out of decisions I should be part of.\n\nSo yes, in theory, evidence could change my mind. I’m not saying I would ignore something right in front of me. But it would have to be real evidence, not just someone reassuring me or telling me I’m overreacting. Because from what I’ve seen, this has been too consistent to just dismiss.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any particularly stressful or traumatic events in the recent past?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, I don’t know if I would call it traumatic exactly, but it has definitely been stressful—very stressful. This all really started about 11 months ago, when my sister’s health suddenly got worse and I had to start coordinating her care.\n\nBefore that, things weren’t perfect, but they were manageable. Then suddenly I was the one trying to keep track of appointments, calls, paperwork, bills, who said what, what needed to be done next. It felt like everything was happening at once, and I had to stay on top of it because if I didn’t, things would fall through the cracks.\n\nSo yes, the recent past has been stressful. Not one single dramatic incident, but the whole situation with my sister getting worse and me having to step in—it’s been going on for nearly a year now, and it’s worn me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about being deceived and targeted would still be true even if there was no evidence to support them?\n\nPatient: If there were truly no evidence at all, I suppose I’d have to look at that. I’m not trying to believe something just because it upsets me or because I want someone to blame. That’s not what this is.\n\nBut from my point of view, there is evidence. It may not be the kind of evidence other people accept, like a written confession or someone admitting, “Yes, we kept you out of it.” But I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the same pattern. My sister’s appointments get moved and somehow I’m not told clearly. Bills show up wrong or confusing. People say they’ll call me back and then they “forget,” or they claim they never got the message. And then when I ask questions, I’m treated like I’m being difficult.\n\nSo when you say “if there was no evidence,” I have trouble with that, because to me the pattern is the evidence. One mistake, fine. Two mistakes, maybe. But when it keeps happening and it always ends with me not having the information I need, or being pushed out of decisions I should be part of, then I can’t just pretend that means nothing.\n\nCould I be wrong about some individual detail? Yes, maybe. But I don’t think I’m wrong about the overall thing. Even if everyone keeps telling me it’s just mistakes, I know what it feels like when information is being kept from me.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your beliefs about being deceived and targeted are something that you might reconsider if new information came to light?\n\nPatient: Yes, I mean… I would like to think I could reconsider something if there was real new information. I’m not trying to be unreasonable. If someone showed me clearly that I misunderstood, or there was a full explanation that actually fit everything, then I’d have to at least look at it.\n\nBut that’s the problem—it never really explains the whole pattern. For months now, I’ve been seeing the same things happen over and over. My sister’s appointments get moved and somehow I’m not told properly. Bills come in wrong, or confusing, or with things that don’t add up. People say they’ll call me back and then they don’t, and later it’s “Oh, I forgot,” or “We never got that message.” And when I press for answers, suddenly I’m the one being treated like I’m difficult.\n\nSo if new information came to light, yes, I’d listen. But it would have to be more than people just saying, “These things happen” or “It’s just mistakes.” Because from where I’m sitting, it’s not random. It keeps ending the same way—with information being kept from me and me being pushed out of decisions I should be included in. So I can say I’d reconsider, but honestly, it would take something pretty solid.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't think people are trying to deceive or target you?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes… I mean, I’m not walking around every single second thinking everyone is out to get me. If I’m doing something ordinary, like making dinner or watching something on television, I’m not necessarily thinking about it the whole time.\n\nBut when it comes to my sister’s care, or the bills, or the phone calls, or anything involving those decisions, it comes right back. Because that’s where the pattern is. Her appointments get changed and I’m not told properly, or a bill shows up that doesn’t make sense, or someone says they’ll call me back and then they don’t, and later it’s treated like some harmless mistake. And maybe one thing could be a mistake, but it’s been months of this.\n\nSo I do have moments when I’m not focused on it, but I can’t honestly say I stop believing it. It’s more like it’s in the background until something happens again and then I think, see, there it is again. Everyone can say it’s just confusion or bad communication, but from my side, it feels very clear that information is being kept from me and that I’m being pushed out of decisions I should be part of.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being deceived and targeted would change if everyone involved, including your sister and the people at the offices, told you directly that there was no intention to deceive or harm you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, if every single person said that to me directly, I would hear it. I’m not saying I’d put my hands over my ears and refuse to listen. If my sister looked me in the eye and the people at the offices all said, “No, there was no intention to deceive you or hurt you,” I would take that seriously, because of course I don’t want this to be true.\n\nBut I don’t think just saying it would erase what I’ve been seeing. That’s the part people don’t seem to understand. I’m here because for months now it’s been the same pattern: my sister’s appointments get moved and somehow I’m not clearly told, bills show up wrong or confusing, people tell me they’ll call me back and then they don’t, and later it’s always “Oh, I forgot,” or “That was just a mistake.” And somehow every one of these “mistakes” ends with me being left out or not having the information I need.\n\nSo if everyone denied it, I’d probably think… well, of course they’re going to deny it. I don’t mean that to sound harsh, but people don’t usually admit they’re keeping someone out of decisions. They explain it away. They make it sound like you’re overreacting. And then I start doubting myself for a minute, but then another thing happens and it all fits the same pattern again.\n\nSo no, I don’t think my belief would just change because they all said there was no intention. It would take more than reassurance. I’d need to see the behavior actually change—being told things clearly, bills making sense, calls returned, no more being pushed aside. Until then, I can’t just accept that it’s all innocent mistakes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being deceived and targeted are something that you are completely convinced of and cannot change, no matter what happens?\n\nPatient: Completely convinced… I don’t know if I like saying it that way, because it makes me sound like I’m unreasonable or like nothing could ever matter. I don’t think of myself that way. I’ve worked in offices for years, I know mistakes happen, I know calls get missed and paperwork gets entered wrong. I understand that.\n\nBut at this point, after months of the same kinds of things happening, yes, I am convinced that something is going on. It’s not just one appointment being moved or one bill being confusing or one person forgetting to call me back. It’s the way it keeps happening, and it always seems to end with me not having the information I need, or me being pushed out of decisions involving my sister.\n\nPeople keep telling me, “Oh, it was just a mistake,” or “No one meant anything by it,” but that doesn’t really answer the pattern. If it were innocent, why does it keep going the same direction? Why am I always the one finding out late, or having to chase people down, or being made to feel like I’m interfering when I ask questions?\n\nSo… could it change? I suppose if things actually changed—if information was given clearly, if the bills made sense, if people followed through and stopped shutting me out—then maybe over time I’d have to reconsider it. But right now, no, I can’t just drop it because people say I should. From what I’ve seen, I know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out of decisions.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being deceived and targeted will ever completely go away?\n\nPatient: Completely go away? I don’t know. I’d like it to, honestly. I’m tired of thinking about it, tired of having to check and recheck everything, tired of feeling like I’m the only one paying attention. It would be much easier if I could believe it was all just normal office mistakes and miscommunication.\n\nBut right now, I can’t see it just disappearing. I’m here because for months it’s been the same pattern. My sister’s appointments get moved and somehow I’m not told properly. Bills show up wrong or confusing. People say they’ll call me back and then they “forget.” And every time, the result is the same: I’m left out, or I’m late getting information, or I’m made to feel like I’m being difficult for asking basic questions.\n\nSo maybe if things actually changed for a long time—if people told me things clearly, if the bills made sense, if calls were returned when they said they would be, if I wasn’t pushed out of decisions—then maybe it would fade. I’m not saying I want to carry this around forever. But as long as the same things keep happening and everyone keeps brushing it off as innocent mistakes, no, I don’t think it will completely go away. Because to me it’s not just a feeling. It’s what I keep seeing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean… I want to say yes, because I don’t want to be the sort of person who says nothing could ever change my mind. That sounds stubborn, and I don’t think I’m stubborn. I’ve worked in offices, I know how paperwork goes, I know mistakes happen. I know people forget things and bills can be entered wrong. So in theory, yes, I suppose there could be information that would make me reconsider.

But it would have to be more than people just telling me, “No one is trying to deceive you.” That’s what keeps happening already. They say it’s just a mistake, just a misunderstanding, just someone forgetting to call back. And maybe if it were one thing, I could accept that. But I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the same pattern—my sister’s appointments get moved and I’m not clearly told, bills show up wrong or confusing, people say they’ll call me back and then they don’t—and every time I’m the one left without the information or made to feel like I’m pushing too hard.

So what would change my mind? I guess if there were complete clarity for a long time. If appointments weren’t changed without me knowing, if the bills made sense the first time, if people actually followed through, if I wasn’t brushed off when I asked questions. Maybe if I saw that consistently, not just for a day or two, I’d start to believe it wasn’t intentional.

But right now, with the way it’s been, I know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out of decisions. So I can’t say one conversation or one explanation would completely change my mind. It would take the pattern stopping.
=====================================

Honestly… I don’t think so.\n\nThat’s the part that’s hard to explain without sounding like I’m being stubborn. And every time I raise it, I’m told it’s just mistakes, or confusion, or I’m overreacting. But it always ends up with me not having the information I need, and me being pushed out of decisions I should be part of.\n\nSo, no, I’m not saying I have some signed document proving it. But I do know what I’m seeing. Maybe I could be wrong about one bill, or one phone call, or one appointment change. But all of it together? Over and over? I really don’t think I’m wrong about that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any situation or evidence that could change your mind about people trying to deceive or target you?\n\nPatient: I suppose if everything suddenly became completely transparent—if every appointment change was documented clearly, if the bills made sense, if people actually returned calls when they said they would, and if I was included in the decisions instead of finding things out afterward—then maybe I’d have to reconsider it.\n\nBut that’s not what has been happening. For months now it’s been the same pattern. My sister’s appointments get moved and I’m not told properly, bills come in wrong, people “forget” to call me back or say they never got the message, and then when I question it, I’m treated like I’m making something out of nothing. And everyone keeps saying, “Oh, it’s just mistakes,” but the mistakes always seem to work in the same direction. This all really started about 11 months ago, when my sister’s health suddenly got worse and I had to start coordinating her care.\n\nBefore that, things weren’t perfect, but they were manageable. If I’m doing something ordinary, like making dinner or watching something on television, I’m not necessarily thinking about it the whole time.\n\nBut when it comes to my sister’s care, or the bills, or the phone calls, or anything involving those decisions, it comes right back. Because that’s where the pattern is. I’m not saying I’d put my hands over my ears and refuse to listen. If my sister looked me in the eye and the people at the offices all said, “No, there was no intention to deceive you or hurt you,” I would take that seriously, because of course I don’t want this to be true.\n\nBut I don’t think just saying it would erase what I’ve been seeing. That’s the part people don’t seem to understand. I don’t mean that to sound harsh, but people don’t usually admit they’re keeping someone out of decisions. They explain it away. They make it sound like you’re overreacting. And then I start doubting myself for a minute, but then another thing happens and it all fits the same pattern again.\n\nSo no, I don’t think my belief would just change because they all said there was no intention. It would take more than reassurance. I’d need to see the behavior actually change—being told things clearly, bills making sense, calls returned, no more being pushed aside. Until then, I can’t just accept that it’s all innocent mistakes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being deceived and targeted are something that you are completely convinced of and cannot change, no matter what happens?\n\nPatient: Completely convinced… I don’t know if I like saying it that way, because it makes me sound like I’m unreasonable or like nothing could ever matter. I don’t think of myself that way. I’ve worked in offices for years, I know mistakes happen, I know calls get missed and paperwork gets entered wrong. I understand that.\n\nBut at this point, after months of the same kinds of things happening, yes, I am convinced that something is going on. It’s not just one appointment being moved or one bill being confusing or one person forgetting to call me back. It’s the way it keeps happening, and it always seems to end with me not having the information I need, or me being pushed out of decisions involving my sister.\n\nPeople keep telling me, “Oh, it was just a mistake,” or “No one meant anything by it,” but that doesn’t really answer the pattern. If it were innocent, why does it keep going the same direction? Why am I always the one finding out late, or having to chase people down, or being made to feel like I’m interfering when I ask questions?\n\nSo… could it change? I don’t know. I’d like it to, honestly. I’m tired of thinking about it, tired of having to check and recheck everything, tired of feeling like I’m the only one paying attention. It would be much easier if I could believe it was all just normal office mistakes and miscommunication.\n\nBut right now, I can’t see it just disappearing. I’m here because for months it’s been the same pattern. My sister’s appointments get moved and somehow I’m not told properly. Bills show up wrong or confusing. People say they’ll call me back and then they “forget.” And every time, the result is the same: I’m left out, or I’m late getting information, or I’m made to feel like I’m being difficult for asking basic questions.\n\nSo maybe if things actually changed for a long time—if people told me things clearly, if the bills made sense, if calls were returned when they said they would be, if I wasn’t pushed out of decisions—then maybe it would fade. I’m not saying I want to carry this around forever. But as long as the same things keep happening and everyone keeps brushing it off as innocent mistakes, no, I don’t think it will completely go away. Because to me it’s not just a feeling. It’s what I keep seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen, or any information you could receive, that would completely change your mind about people trying to deceive or target you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I want to say yes, because I don’t want to be the sort of person who says nothing could ever change my mind. That sounds stubborn, and I don’t think I’m stubborn. I’ve worked in offices, I know how paperwork goes, I know mistakes happen. I know people forget things and bills can be entered wrong. For months now, I’ve been seeing the same things happen over and over. My sister’s appointments get moved and somehow I’m not told properly. Bills come in wrong, or confusing, or with things that don’t add up. People say they’ll call me back and then they don’t, and later it’s “Oh, I forgot,” or “We never got that message.” And when I press for answers, suddenly I’m the one being treated like I’m difficult.\n\nSo if new information came to light, yes, I’d listen. But it would have to be more than people just saying, “These things happen” or “It’s just mistakes.” Because from where I’m sitting, it’s not random. It keeps ending the same way—with information being kept from me and me being pushed out of decisions I should be included in. So I can say I’d reconsider, but honestly, it would take something pretty solid.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't think people are trying to deceive or target you?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes… I mean, I’m not walking around every single second thinking everyone is out to get me. If I’m doing something ordinary, like making dinner or watching something on television, I’m not necessarily thinking about it the whole time.\n\nBut when it comes to my sister’s care, or the bills, or the phone calls, or anything involving those decisions, it comes right back. Because that’s where the pattern is. Her appointments get changed and I’m not told properly, or a bill shows up that doesn’t make sense, or someone says they’ll call me back and then they don’t, and later it’s treated like some harmless mistake. And maybe one thing could be a mistake, but it’s been months of this.\n\nSo I do have moments when I’m not focused on it, but I can’t honestly say I stop believing it. It’s more like it’s in the background until something happens again and then I think, see, there it is again. Everyone can say it’s just confusion or bad communication, but from my side, it feels very clear that information is being kept from me and that I’m being pushed out of decisions I should be part of.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being deceived and targeted would change if everyone involved, including your sister and the people at the offices, told you directly that there was no intention to deceive or harm you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, if every single person said that to me directly, I would hear it. I’m not saying I’d put my hands over my ears and refuse to listen. If my sister looked me in the eye and the people at the offices all said, “No, there was no intention to deceive you or hurt you,” I would take that seriously, because of course I don’t want this to be true.\n\nBut I don’t think just saying it would erase what I’ve been seeing. That’s the part people don’t seem to understand. I’m here because for months now it’s been the same pattern: my sister’s appointments get moved and somehow I’m not clearly told, bills show up wrong or confusing, people tell me they’ll call me back and then they don’t, and later it’s always “Oh, I forgot,” or “That was just a mistake.” And somehow every one of these “mistakes” ends with me being left out or not having the information I need.\n\nSo if everyone denied it, I’d probably think… well, of course they’re going to deny it. I don’t mean that to sound harsh, but people don’t usually admit they’re keeping someone out of decisions. They explain it away. They make it sound like you’re overreacting. And then I start doubting myself for a minute, but then another thing happens and it all fits the same pattern again.\n\nSo no, I don’t think my belief would just change because they all said there was no intention. It would take more than reassurance. I’d need to see the behavior actually change—being told things clearly, bills making sense, calls returned, no more being pushed aside. Until then, I can’t just accept that it’s all innocent mistakes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being deceived and targeted are something that you are completely convinced of and cannot change, no matter what happens?\n\nPatient: Completely convinced… I don’t know if I like saying it that way, because it makes me sound like I’m unreasonable or like nothing could ever matter. I don’t think of myself that way. I’ve worked in offices for years, I know mistakes happen, I know calls get missed and paperwork gets entered wrong. I understand that.\n\nBut at this point, after months of the same kinds of things happening, yes, I am convinced that something is going on. It’s not just one appointment being moved or one bill being confusing or one person forgetting to call me back. It’s the way it keeps happening, and it always seems to end with me not having the information I need, or me being pushed out of decisions involving my sister.\n\nPeople keep telling me, “Oh, it was just a mistake,” or “No one meant anything by it,” but that doesn’t really answer the pattern. If it were innocent, why does it keep going the same direction? Why am I always the one finding out late, or having to chase people down, or being made to feel like I’m interfering when I ask questions?\n\nSo… could it change? I suppose if things actually changed—if information was given clearly, if the bills made sense, if people followed through and stopped shutting me out—then maybe over time I’d have to reconsider it. But right now, no, I can’t just drop it because people say I should. From what I’ve seen, I know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out of decisions.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being deceived and targeted will ever completely go away?\n\nPatient: Completely go away? I don’t know. I’d like it to, honestly. I’m tired of thinking about it, tired of having to check and recheck everything, tired of feeling like I’m the only one paying attention. It would be much easier if I could believe it was all just normal office mistakes and miscommunication.\n\nBut right now, I can’t see it just disappearing. I’m here because for months it’s been the same pattern. My sister’s appointments get moved and somehow I’m not told properly. Bills show up wrong or confusing. People say they’ll call me back and then they “forget.” And every time, the result is the same: I’m left out, or I’m late getting information, or I’m made to feel like I’m being difficult for asking basic questions.\n\nSo maybe if things actually changed for a long time—if people told me things clearly, if the bills made sense, if calls were returned when they said they would be, if I wasn’t pushed out of decisions—then maybe it would fade. I’m not saying I want to carry this around forever. But as long as the same things keep happening and everyone keeps brushing it off as innocent mistakes, no, I don’t think it will completely go away. Because to me it’s not just a feeling. It’s what I keep seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen, or any information you could receive, that would completely change your mind about people trying to deceive or target you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I want to say yes, because I don’t want to be the sort of person who says nothing could ever change my mind. That sounds stubborn, and I don’t think I’m stubborn. I’ve worked in offices, I know how paperwork goes, I know mistakes happen. I know people forget things and bills can be entered wrong. So in theory, yes, I suppose there could be information that would make me reconsider.\n\nBut it would have to be more than people just telling me, “No one is trying to deceive you.” That’s what keeps happening already. They say it’s just a mistake, just a misunderstanding, just someone forgetting to call back. And maybe if it were one thing, I could accept that. But I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the same pattern—my sister’s appointments get moved and I’m not clearly told, bills show up wrong or confusing, people say they’ll call me back and then they don’t—and every time I’m the one left without the information or made to feel like I’m pushing too hard.\n\nSo what would change my mind? I guess if there were complete clarity for a long time. If appointments weren’t changed without me knowing, if the bills made sense the first time, if people actually followed through, if I wasn’t brushed off when I asked questions. Maybe if I saw that consistently, not just for a day or two, I’d start to believe it wasn’t intentional.\n\nBut right now, with the way it’s been, I know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out of decisions. So I can’t say one conversation or one explanation would completely change my mind. It would take the pattern stopping.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

{"intent": "Determine the presence of delusional thinking", "question": "Do you think your beliefs about being deceived and targeted are based on reality or are they something that you've come to believe despite a lack of evidence?"}

Based on reality. I mean, that’s how I see it. I know you’re asking whether I believe this without evidence, but I don’t think that’s fair to say, because to me the evidence is the pattern. It’s not some idea I woke up with one morning for no reason.

I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the same kinds of things happen over and over. My sister’s appointments get moved, and somehow I’m not told clearly or I find out after the fact. Bills show up wrong or confusing, and then when I ask about them, nobody can give me a straight answer. People say they’ll call me back, and then they “forget,” or they act like I’m bothering them for following up. And it always ends the same way: I’m left out of the information, or I’m put in a position where I look unreasonable for asking basic questions.

So when people say, “Well, where’s your proof?” I don’t know what kind of proof they expect me to have. I’m not sitting there recording every phone call or collecting signed statements. But I’ve worked in offices. I know the difference between one mistake and a repeated pattern where things keep benefiting everyone except me. If it were just random, it wouldn’t keep landing the same way.

Everyone keeps telling me it’s just mistakes, but I don’t believe that anymore. I know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out of decisions. That’s not something I’m believing in spite of what’s happening. It’s because of what’s happening.

[
  {
    "role": "system",

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF22, F60.0, F32.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I’m here because this has been going on for months now, and I feel like nobody is taking it seriously. It’s this pattern I keep seeing—little things, but not really little when you put them all together. My sister’s appointments get moved around and I’m not told properly, or I find out after the fact. Bills show up with things wrong on them, amounts or details that don’t make sense. People say they’re going to call me back and then they “forget,” or they act like they never got the message.\n\nAnd everyone keeps telling me it’s just mistakes, or that offices are busy, or that I’m reading too much into it, but that’s not what it feels like. It’s too consistent. I feel like information is being kept from me, like people are trying to leave me out and push me out of decisions I should be involved in. I’m not saying I have every piece of proof lined up in a folder, but I know what I’m seeing, and I know when something isn’t right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people are intentionally trying to harm or deceive you in these situations?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean… I don’t know if “harm” is the word I would use exactly, not like someone is physically coming after me, but deceive? Keep me out? Yes. That’s what it feels like, and it’s been happening too consistently for me to believe it’s all innocent.\n\nFor months now it’s been the same pattern. My sister’s appointments get moved and somehow I’m not told properly, or I find out later, like after decisions have already been made. Then there are bills that come in with wrong amounts or strange details, and when I ask about it, people act like it’s just some clerical error. And the phone calls—people say they’ll call back and then they don’t, or they say they never got the message. One thing by itself, fine, maybe it’s a mistake. But when it happens over and over, from different directions, it starts to look like people are controlling what information I get.\n\nSo yes, I do think there’s intention behind it. I think they’re trying to push me out of decisions I should be involved in. Everyone keeps saying I’m reading too much into it, but I know what I’m seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these people are targeting you specifically?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do. I mean, maybe not every single person in every office wakes up thinking about me specifically, but the way it keeps happening, it feels directed at me. It’s always me who gets left out, me who doesn’t get the call back, me who finds out after something has already been changed with my sister’s appointments. And then when I ask questions, suddenly nobody knows anything, or they say it was just a mistake.\n\nThat’s the part people don’t seem to understand. If it happened once or twice, fine, I could accept that. Offices are busy, people make errors. But for months now it’s been the same pattern—appointments moved, bills showing up wrong, messages supposedly not received or forgotten—and somehow all of it has the same effect: I’m kept from having the information I need, and I’m pushed out of decisions I should be involved in.\n\nSo yes, I think I’m being targeted in that sense. Maybe they wouldn’t use that word, but that’s what it feels like to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs you have about being deceived and targeted are based on facts that other people would agree with if they saw the same evidence?\n\nPatient: Yes, I do think it’s based on facts. That’s what frustrates me, because people act like I’m just “feeling” something or being suspicious for no reason, but I’m talking about actual things that happened. My sister’s appointments have been moved, and I haven’t been told properly. Bills have come in with wrong amounts or details that don’t line up. People have said they would call me back and then they don’t, or they say they never got a message. Those are facts.\n\nNow, would other people agree with me about what it all means? That’s where everyone keeps backing away. They’ll say, “Oh, that could just be a mistake,” or “Offices get busy,” or “Maybe there was a misunderstanding.” But if they saw the whole pattern, not just one isolated incident, I think they would have to admit something is going on. Because it’s not one thing. It’s been months of the same kind of thing happening over and over, and the result is always the same: I’m kept out of the loop, I don’t get the information I need, and I’m pushed away from decisions I should be part of.\n\nSo yes, I believe if someone really looked at all of it together, honestly, they’d see what I’m seeing. They might not want to say it out loud, but they’d see it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs of yours could be wrong, or are you absolutely certain they are true?\n\nPatient: I wouldn’t say absolutely certain in the way people mean, like I can prove every single person’s motive on paper. I know I can’t climb inside someone’s head. But could I be wrong about the overall pattern? Honestly… I don’t think so.\n\nThat’s the part that’s hard to explain without sounding like I’m being stubborn. I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the same things happen again and again—my sister’s appointments get moved, bills show up wrong, people say they’ll call me back and then they “forget” or suddenly never got the message. And every time I raise it, I’m told it’s just mistakes, or confusion, or I’m overreacting. But it always ends up with me not having the information I need, and me being pushed out of decisions I should be part of.\n\nSo, no, I’m not saying I have some signed document proving it. But I do know what I’m seeing. Maybe I could be wrong about one bill, or one phone call, or one appointment change. But all of it together? Over and over? I really don’t think I’m wrong about that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any situation or evidence that could change your mind about people trying to deceive or target you?\n\nPatient: I suppose if everything suddenly became completely transparent—if every appointment change was documented clearly, if the bills made sense, if people actually returned calls when they said they would, and if I was included in the decisions instead of finding things out afterward—then maybe I’d have to reconsider it.\n\nBut that’s not what has been happening. For months now it’s been the same pattern. My sister’s appointments get moved and I’m not told properly, bills come in wrong, people “forget” to call me back or say they never got the message, and then when I question it, I’m treated like I’m making something out of nothing. And everyone keeps saying, “Oh, it’s just mistakes,” but the mistakes always seem to work in the same direction. They keep information from me and push me out of decisions I should be part of.\n\nSo yes, in theory, evidence could change my mind. I’m not saying I would ignore something right in front of me. But it would have to be real evidence, not just someone reassuring me or telling me I’m overreacting. Because from what I’ve seen, this has been too consistent to just dismiss.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any particularly stressful or traumatic events in the recent past?\n\nPatient: Yes. I mean, I don’t know if I would call it traumatic exactly, but it has definitely been stressful—very stressful. This all really started about 11 months ago, when my sister’s health suddenly got worse and I had to start coordinating her care.\n\nBefore that, things weren’t perfect, but they were manageable. Then suddenly I was the one trying to keep track of appointments, calls, paperwork, bills, who said what, what needed to be done next. It felt like everything was happening at once, and I had to stay on top of it because if I didn’t, things would fall through the cracks.\n\nSo yes, the recent past has been stressful. Not one single dramatic incident, but the whole situation with my sister getting worse and me having to step in—it’s been going on for nearly a year now, and it’s worn me down.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about being deceived and targeted would still be true even if there was no evidence to support them?\n\nPatient: If there were truly no evidence at all, I suppose I’d have to look at that. I’m not trying to believe something just because it upsets me or because I want someone to blame. That’s not what this is.\n\nBut from my point of view, there is evidence. It may not be the kind of evidence other people accept, like a written confession or someone admitting, “Yes, we kept you out of it.” But I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the same pattern. My sister’s appointments get moved and somehow I’m not told clearly. Bills show up wrong or confusing. People say they’ll call me back and then they “forget,” or they claim they never got the message. And then when I ask questions, I’m treated like I’m being difficult.\n\nSo when you say “if there was no evidence,” I have trouble with that, because to me the pattern is the evidence. One mistake, fine. Two mistakes, maybe. But when it keeps happening and it always ends with me not having the information I need, or being pushed out of decisions I should be part of, then I can’t just pretend that means nothing.\n\nCould I be wrong about some individual detail? Yes, maybe. But I don’t think I’m wrong about the overall thing. Even if everyone keeps telling me it’s just mistakes, I know what it feels like when information is being kept from me.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your beliefs about being deceived and targeted are something that you might reconsider if new information came to light?\n\nPatient: Yes, I mean… I would like to think I could reconsider something if there was real new information. I’m not trying to be unreasonable. If someone showed me clearly that I misunderstood, or there was a full explanation that actually fit everything, then I’d have to at least look at it.\n\nBut that’s the problem—it never really explains the whole pattern. For months now, I’ve been seeing the same things happen over and over. My sister’s appointments get moved and somehow I’m not told properly. Bills come in wrong, or confusing, or with things that don’t add up. People say they’ll call me back and then they don’t, and later it’s “Oh, I forgot,” or “We never got that message.” And when I press for answers, suddenly I’m the one being treated like I’m difficult.\n\nSo if new information came to light, yes, I’d listen. But it would have to be more than people just saying, “These things happen” or “It’s just mistakes.” Because from where I’m sitting, it’s not random. It keeps ending the same way—with information being kept from me and me being pushed out of decisions I should be included in. So I can say I’d reconsider, but honestly, it would take something pretty solid.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times when you don't think people are trying to deceive or target you?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes… I mean, I’m not walking around every single second thinking everyone is out to get me. If I’m doing something ordinary, like making dinner or watching something on television, I’m not necessarily thinking about it the whole time.\n\nBut when it comes to my sister’s care, or the bills, or the phone calls, or anything involving those decisions, it comes right back. Because that’s where the pattern is. Her appointments get changed and I’m not told properly, or a bill shows up that doesn’t make sense, or someone says they’ll call me back and then they don’t, and later it’s treated like some harmless mistake. And maybe one thing could be a mistake, but it’s been months of this.\n\nSo I do have moments when I’m not focused on it, but I can’t honestly say I stop believing it. It’s more like it’s in the background until something happens again and then I think, see, there it is again. Everyone can say it’s just confusion or bad communication, but from my side, it feels very clear that information is being kept from me and that I’m being pushed out of decisions I should be part of.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being deceived and targeted would change if everyone involved, including your sister and the people at the offices, told you directly that there was no intention to deceive or harm you?\n\nPatient: Honestly, if every single person said that to me directly, I would hear it. I’m not saying I’d put my hands over my ears and refuse to listen. If my sister looked me in the eye and the people at the offices all said, “No, there was no intention to deceive you or hurt you,” I would take that seriously, because of course I don’t want this to be true.\n\nBut I don’t think just saying it would erase what I’ve been seeing. That’s the part people don’t seem to understand. I’m here because for months now it’s been the same pattern: my sister’s appointments get moved and somehow I’m not clearly told, bills show up wrong or confusing, people tell me they’ll call me back and then they don’t, and later it’s always “Oh, I forgot,” or “That was just a mistake.” And somehow every one of these “mistakes” ends with me being left out or not having the information I need.\n\nSo if everyone denied it, I’d probably think… well, of course they’re going to deny it. I don’t mean that to sound harsh, but people don’t usually admit they’re keeping someone out of decisions. They explain it away. They make it sound like you’re overreacting. And then I start doubting myself for a minute, but then another thing happens and it all fits the same pattern again.\n\nSo no, I don’t think my belief would just change because they all said there was no intention. It would take more than reassurance. I’d need to see the behavior actually change—being told things clearly, bills making sense, calls returned, no more being pushed aside. Until then, I can’t just accept that it’s all innocent mistakes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being deceived and targeted are something that you are completely convinced of and cannot change, no matter what happens?\n\nPatient: Completely convinced… I don’t know if I like saying it that way, because it makes me sound like I’m unreasonable or like nothing could ever matter. I don’t think of myself that way. I’ve worked in offices for years, I know mistakes happen, I know calls get missed and paperwork gets entered wrong. I understand that.\n\nBut at this point, after months of the same kinds of things happening, yes, I am convinced that something is going on. It’s not just one appointment being moved or one bill being confusing or one person forgetting to call me back. It’s the way it keeps happening, and it always seems to end with me not having the information I need, or me being pushed out of decisions involving my sister.\n\nPeople keep telling me, “Oh, it was just a mistake,” or “No one meant anything by it,” but that doesn’t really answer the pattern. If it were innocent, why does it keep going the same direction? Why am I always the one finding out late, or having to chase people down, or being made to feel like I’m interfering when I ask questions?\n\nSo… could it change? I suppose if things actually changed—if information was given clearly, if the bills made sense, if people followed through and stopped shutting me out—then maybe over time I’d have to reconsider it. But right now, no, I can’t just drop it because people say I should. From what I’ve seen, I know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out of decisions.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being deceived and targeted will ever completely go away?\n\nPatient: Completely go away? I don’t know. I’d like it to, honestly. I’m tired of thinking about it, tired of having to check and recheck everything, tired of feeling like I’m the only one paying attention. It would be much easier if I could believe it was all just normal office mistakes and miscommunication.\n\nBut right now, I can’t see it just disappearing. I’m here because for months it’s been the same pattern. My sister’s appointments get moved and somehow I’m not told properly. Bills show up wrong or confusing. People say they’ll call me back and then they “forget.” And every time, the result is the same: I’m left out, or I’m late getting information, or I’m made to feel like I’m being difficult for asking basic questions.\n\nSo maybe if things actually changed for a long time—if people told me things clearly, if the bills made sense, if calls were returned when they said they would be, if I wasn’t pushed out of decisions—then maybe it would fade. I’m not saying I want to carry this around forever. But as long as the same things keep happening and everyone keeps brushing it off as innocent mistakes, no, I don’t think it will completely go away. Because to me it’s not just a feeling. It’s what I keep seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen, or any information you could receive, that would completely change your mind about people trying to deceive or target you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I want to say yes, because I don’t want to be the sort of person who says nothing could ever change my mind. That sounds stubborn, and I don’t think I’m stubborn. I’ve worked in offices, I know how paperwork goes, I know mistakes happen. I know people forget things and bills can be entered wrong. So in theory, yes, I suppose there could be information that would make me reconsider.\n\nBut it would have to be more than people just telling me, “No one is trying to deceive you.” That’s what keeps happening already. They say it’s just a mistake, just a misunderstanding, just someone forgetting to call back. And maybe if it were one thing, I could accept that. But I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the same pattern—my sister’s appointments get moved and I’m not clearly told, bills show up wrong or confusing, people say they’ll call me back and then they don’t—and every time I’m the one left without the information or made to feel like I’m pushing too hard.\n\nSo what would change my mind? I guess if there were complete clarity for a long time. If appointments weren’t changed without me knowing, if the bills made sense the first time, if people actually followed through, if I wasn’t brushed off when I asked questions. Maybe if I saw that consistently, not just for a day or two, I’d start to believe it wasn’t intentional.\n\nBut right now, with the way it’s been, I know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out of decisions. So I can’t say one conversation or one explanation would completely change my mind. It would take the pattern stopping.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being deceived and targeted are based on reality or are they something that you've come to believe despite a lack of evidence?\n\nPatient: Based on reality. I mean, that’s how I see it. I know you’re asking whether I believe this without evidence, but I don’t think that’s fair to say, because to me the evidence is the pattern. It’s not some idea I woke up with one morning for no reason.\n\nI’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the same kinds of things happen over and over. My sister’s appointments get moved, and somehow I’m not told clearly or I find out after the fact. Bills show up wrong or confusing, and then when I ask about them, nobody can give me a straight answer. People say they’ll call me back, and then they “forget,” or they act like I’m bothering them for following up. And it always ends the same way: I’m left out of the information, or I’m put in a position where I look unreasonable for asking basic questions.\n\nSo when people say, “Well, where’s your proof?” I don’t know what kind of proof they expect me to have. I’m not sitting there recording every phone call or collecting signed statements. But I’ve worked in offices. I know the difference between one mistake and a repeated pattern where things keep benefiting everyone except me. If it were just random, it wouldn’t keep landing the same way.\n\nEveryone keeps telling me it’s just mistakes, but I don’t believe that anymore. I know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out of decisions. That’s not something I’m believing in spite of what’s happening. It’s because of what’s happening.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Until then, I can’t just accept that it’s all innocent mistakes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being deceived and targeted are something that you are completely convinced of and cannot change, no matter what happens?\n\nPatient: Completely convinced… I don’t know if I like saying it that way, because it makes me sound like I’m unreasonable or like nothing could ever matter. I don’t think of myself that way. I’ve worked in offices for years, I know mistakes happen, I know calls get missed and paperwork gets entered wrong. I understand that.\n\nBut at this point, after months of the same kinds of things happening, yes, I am convinced that something is going on. It’s not just one appointment being moved or one bill being confusing or one person forgetting to call me back. It’s the way it keeps happening, and it always seems to end with me not having the information I need, or me being pushed out of decisions involving my sister.\n\nPeople keep telling me, “Oh, it was just a mistake,” or “No one meant anything by it,” but that doesn’t really answer the pattern. If it were innocent, why does it keep going the same direction? Why am I always the one finding out late, or having to chase people down, or being made to feel like I’m interfering when I ask questions?\n\nSo… could it change? I suppose if things actually changed—if information was given clearly, if the bills made sense, if people followed through and stopped shutting me out—then maybe over time I’d have to reconsider it. But right now, no, I can’t just drop it because people say I should. From what I’ve seen, I know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out of decisions.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being deceived and targeted will ever completely go away?\n\nPatient: Completely go away? I don’t know. I’d like it to, honestly. I’m tired of thinking about it, tired of having to check and recheck everything, tired of feeling like I’m the only one paying attention. It would be much easier if I could believe it was all just normal office mistakes and miscommunication.\n\nBut right now, I can’t see it just disappearing. I’m here because for months it’s been the same pattern. My sister’s appointments get moved and somehow I’m not told properly. Bills show up wrong or confusing. People say they’ll call me back and then they “forget.” And every time, the result is the same: I’m left out, or I’m late getting information, or I’m made to feel like I’m being difficult for asking basic questions.\n\nSo maybe if things actually changed for a long time—if people told me things clearly, if the bills made sense, if calls were returned when they said they would be, if I wasn’t pushed out of decisions—then maybe it would fade. I’m not saying I want to carry this around forever. But as long as the same things keep happening and everyone keeps brushing it off as innocent mistakes, no, I don’t think it will completely go away. Because to me it’s not just a feeling. It’s what I keep seeing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is anything that could happen, or any information you could receive, that would completely change your mind about people trying to deceive or target you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I want to say yes, because I don’t want to be the sort of person who says nothing could ever change my mind. That sounds stubborn, and I don’t think I’m stubborn. I’ve worked in offices, I know how paperwork goes, I know mistakes happen. I know people forget things and bills can be entered wrong. So in theory, yes, I suppose there could be information that would make me reconsider.\n\nBut it would have to be more than people just telling me, “No one is trying to deceive you.” That’s what keeps happening already. They say it’s just a mistake, just a misunderstanding, just someone forgetting to call back. And maybe if it were one thing, I could accept that. But I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the same pattern—my sister’s appointments get moved and I’m not clearly told, bills show up wrong or confusing, people say they’ll call me back and then they don’t—and every time I’m the one left without the information or made to feel like I’m pushing too hard.\n\nSo what would change my mind? I guess if there were complete clarity for a long time. If appointments weren’t changed without me knowing, if the bills made sense the first time, if people actually followed through, if I wasn’t brushed off when I asked questions. Maybe if I saw that consistently, not just for a day or two, I’d start to believe it wasn’t intentional.\n\nBut right now, with the way it’s been, I know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out of decisions. So I can’t say one conversation or one explanation would completely change my mind. It would take the pattern stopping.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your beliefs about being deceived and targeted are based on reality or are they something that you've come to believe despite a lack of evidence?\n\nPatient: Based on reality. I mean, that’s how I see it. I know you’re asking whether I believe this without evidence, but I don’t think that’s fair to say, because to me the evidence is the pattern. It’s not some idea I woke up with one morning for no reason.\n\nI’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the same kinds of things happen over and over. My sister’s appointments get moved, and somehow I’m not told clearly or I find out after the fact. Bills show up wrong or confusing, and then when I ask about them, nobody can give me a straight answer. People say they’ll call me back, and then they “forget,” or they act like I’m bothering them for following up. And it always ends the same way: I’m left out of the information, or I’m put in a position where I look unreasonable for asking basic questions.\n\nSo when people say, “Well, where’s your proof?” I don’t know what kind of proof they expect me to have. I’m not sitting there recording every phone call or collecting signed statements. But I’ve worked in offices. I know the difference between one mistake and a repeated pattern where things keep benefiting everyone except me. If it were just random, it wouldn’t keep landing the same way.\n\nEveryone keeps telling me it’s just mistakes, but I don’t believe that anymore. I know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out of decisions. That’s not something I’m believing in spite of what’s happening. It’s because of what’s happening.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Honestly… yes, I think it would still be true, because people telling me my interpretation is wrong doesn’t erase what’s been happening. I know that may sound like I’m not listening, but I am listening. I hear what people say. I hear when they tell me, “These are just mistakes,” or “You’re reading too much into it,” or “No one is trying to leave you out.” But the problem is, the same things keep happening over and over.

I’m here because for months now I’ve been seeing the same pattern. My sister’s appointments get moved and somehow I’m not told properly. The bills come in wrong or confusing, and then I’m the one who has to chase people down for an explanation. People say they’ll call me back, and then they “forget,” and somehow that always means I’m left without the information I need. And when I ask questions, I’m treated like I’m being unreasonable, like I’m causing trouble just by wanting clear answers.

So if everyone around me said I was wrong, even experts, I’d have to ask: are they looking at the whole pattern, or are they just looking at each incident separately? Because separately, yes, maybe each thing can be explained away. One changed appointment, one wrong bill, one missed phone call. But when it keeps happening and the outcome is always that I’m pushed out of decisions or kept in the dark, then no, I can’t just accept that it’s all innocent.

I’m not trying to be difficult. I would actually like to be wrong, because it’s exhausting. But right now, even if everyone told me my interpretation was incorrect, I’d still know they’re keeping information from me and trying to push me out. That’s what the pattern shows me.
